  4129              return &self.main_agent;
  4130          }
  4131      }{};
  4132      defer app.agent().session_persistence.deinit(alloc);
  4133      defer app.agent().session_title.deinit(alloc);
  4134  
  4135      var idle: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
  4136      defer idle.deinit();

  4141      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, idle.written(), "generation: status=none") != null);
  4142  
  4143      const generation = &app.agent().session_persistence.title_generation;
  4144      generation.last.status = .failed;
  4145      generation.last.reason = .transport_error;
  4146      generation.last.detail = "ConnectionRefused";
  4147      generation.last.elapsed_ms = 15001;
  4148      const model = "openai/gpt-5.6-luna";
  4149      @memcpy(generation.last.model_buf[0..model.len], model);
  4150      generation.last.model_len = model.len;
  4151      const session_id = "abc123sess";
  4152      @memcpy(generation.last.session_buf[0..session_id.len], session_id);
  4153      generation.last.session_len = session_id.len;
  4154  
  4155      var failed: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
  4156      defer failed.deinit();
  4157      try writeSessionTitleSummary(&failed.writer, &app, alloc);
  4158      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, failed.written(), "generation: status=failed session=abc123sess model=openai/gpt-5.6-luna reason=transport_error detail=ConnectionRefused elapsed=15001ms") != null);
  4159  
  4160      generation.last.status = .installed;
  4161      generation.last.reason = null;
  4162      generation.last.detail = "";
  4163      try app.agent().session_title.appendSlice(alloc, "Fix renderer lag");
  4164  
  4165      var installed: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
  4166      defer installed.deinit();
  4167      try writeSessionTitleSummary(&installed.writer, &app, alloc);
  4168      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, installed.written(), "title: Fix renderer lag") != null);
  4169      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, installed.written(), "generation: status=installed") != null);
  4170  }

  4172  test "trace tool calls preserve outcomes and mask obvious secrets" {
  4173      const alloc = std.testing.allocator;
  4174      diagnostics.resetForTest();
  4175      defer diagnostics.resetForTest();
  4176  
  4177      const fixtures = [_]struct {
  4178          name: []const u8,
  4179          outcome: diagnostics.ToolCallOutcome,
  4180          args: []const u8,
  4181          result: []const u8,
  4182          subagent_id: u64 = 0,
  4183      }{
  4184          .{ .name = "read_file", .outcome = .succeeded, .args = "{\"path\":\"README.md\"}", .result = "read ok" },
  4185          .{ .name = "edit_file", .outcome = .rejected, .args = "{}", .result = "not unique" },
  4186          .{ .name = "shell", .outcome = .command_failed, .args = "{}", .result = "exit 7" },
  4187          .{ .name = "read_file", .outcome = .tool_failed, .args = "{}", .result = "missing" },
  4188          .{ .name = "run_command", .outcome = .runtime_failed, .args = "{\"command\":\"AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY=abcdefghijklmnop zig build\"}", .result = "failed with PASSWORD=abcdefghijklmnop", .subagent_id = 3 },
  4189      };
  4190      for (fixtures, 0..) |fixture, index| {
  4191          var call: diagnostics.ToolCallMetric = .{
  4192              .started_at_ms = @intCast(1000 + index),
  4193              .duration_ms = 1,
  4194              .outcome = fixture.outcome,
  4195              .subagent_id = fixture.subagent_id,
  4196          };
  4197          call.setName(fixture.name);
  4198          call.setArgs(fixture.args);
  4199          call.setResult(fixture.result);
  4200          diagnostics.recordToolCall(call);
  4201      }
  4202  
  4203      var out: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
  4204      defer out.deinit();
  4205      try writeToolCallsSummary(&out.writer, alloc, &.{});
  4206      const text = out.written();
  4207  
  4208      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "last=5 succeeded=1 rejected=1 command_failed=1 tool_failed=1 runtime_failed=1") != null);
  4209      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "name=edit_file outcome=rejected") != null);
  4210      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "name=shell outcome=command_failed") != null);
  4211      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "name=read_file outcome=tool_failed") != null);
  4212      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "name=run_command outcome=runtime_failed") != null);
  4213      const non_success_pos = std.mem.find(u8, text, "name=run_command") orelse return error.TestExpectedEqual;
  4214      const success_pos = std.mem.find(u8, text, "name=read_file outcome=succeeded") orelse return error.TestExpectedEqual;
  4215      try std.testing.expect(non_success_pos < success_pos);
  4216      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "source=subagent#3") != null);
  4217      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "abcdefghijklmnop") == null);
  4218      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY=[redacted]") != null);
  4219      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "PASSWORD=[redacted]") != null);
  4220  }

  4368                  "oldest"

  4372                  "middle",

  4375              .provenance = .provider_executed,

  4380          .user = .{ .text = @constCast("search") },

  4382          .execution = .{ .tool_steps = &steps },
  4383      } }};
  4384      var summaries: [diagnostics.tool_call_ring_capacity]ProviderToolCallSummary = undefined;

  4477  test "trace network section reports session totals and window coverage" {
  4478      const alloc = std.testing.allocator;
  4479      diagnostics.resetForTest();
  4480      defer diagnostics.resetForTest();
  4481  
  4482      // More calls than the ring holds so the coverage line must admit eviction.
  4483      var i: u64 = 0;
  4484      while (i < diagnostics.network_ring_capacity + 2) : (i += 1) {
  4485          var call: diagnostics.NetworkCall = .{
  4486              .started_at_ms = 1_000 + @as(i64, @intCast(i)) * 1_000,
  4487              .duration_ms = 100,
  4488              .status = if (i == 0) 500 else 200,
  4489              .turn_id = if (i < 2) 1 else 2,
  4490          };
  4491          call.setModel("openai/gpt-5.6-sol");
  4492          diagnostics.recordNetworkCall(call);
  4493      }
  4494  
  4495      var out: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
  4496      defer out.deinit();
  4497      try writeNetworkCallsSummary(&out.writer);
  4498      const text = out.written();
  4499  
  4500      const total = diagnostics.network_ring_capacity + 2;
  4501      var expect_buf: [160]u8 = undefined;
  4502      const session_line = try std.mem.print(&expect_buf, "session: calls={d} ok={d} errors=1", .{ total, total - 1 });
  4503      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, session_line) != null);
  4504      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "coverage: window holds only the last") != null);
  4505      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "turns:\n") != null);
  4506      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "turn 1: calls=2 errors=1") != null);
  4507      try std.testing.expect(std.mem.find(u8, text, "turn 2: calls=32 errors=0") != null);
  4508  }

  1500  /// Everything a rebuild has to preserve: the tab, the row under the cursor,
  1501  /// the scroll window, and the query.
  1502  const MenuCarry = struct {
  1503      ready: bool,
  1504      tab: ?[]u8,
  1505      selected_id: ?[]u8,
  1506      selected_connection: ?[]u8,
  1507      window: usize,
  1508      selected: usize,
  1509      query_len: usize,
  1510      query_buf: [256]u8,
  1511  
  1512      fn take(alloc: Allocator, menu: *const ModelMenu) !MenuCarry {
  1513          const ready = menu.load_state == .ready;
  1514          var carry: MenuCarry = .{
  1515              .ready = ready,
  1516              .tab = null,
  1517              .selected_id = null,
  1518              .selected_connection = null,
  1519              .window = menu.window_start,
  1520              .selected = menu.selected_index,
  1521              .query_len = menu.query_len,
  1522              .query_buf = undefined,
  1523          };
  1524          @memcpy(carry.query_buf[0..menu.query_len], menu.query());
  1525          errdefer carry.deinit(alloc);
  1526          if (!ready) return carry;

  6723  /// Answers discovery, then holds the token request until the test releases
  6724  /// it, as a slow issuer would. It gives up early only when the request
  6725  /// carries a cancel flag that is set.
  6726  const HeldRefreshTransport = struct {
  6727      token_request_seen: std.atomic.Value(bool) = .init(false),
  6728      token_request_cancellable: std.atomic.Value(bool) = .init(false),
  6729      release: std.atomic.Value(bool) = .init(false),
  6730  
  6731      fn provider(self: *HeldRefreshTransport) oauth_transport.Provider {
  6732          return .{ .context = self, .execute_fn = execute };
  6733      }
  6734  
  6735      fn execute(raw: ?*anyopaque, alloc: Allocator, request: oauth_transport.Request) !oauth_transport.Response {
  6736          const self: *HeldRefreshTransport = @ptrCast(@alignCast(raw.?));
  6737          if (request.method == .get) return .{
  6738              .disposition = .accepted,
  6739              .body = try alloc.dupe(u8, "{\"issuer\":\"https://vercel.com\",\"device_authorization_endpoint\":\"https://vercel.com/oauth/device\",\"token_endpoint\":\"https://vercel.com/oauth/token\"}"),
  6740          };
  6741          self.token_request_cancellable.store(request.cancel_flag != null, .seq_cst);
  6742          self.token_request_seen.store(true, .seq_cst);
  6743          while (!self.release.load(.seq_cst)) {
  6744              if (request.cancel_flag) |flag| if (flag.load(.seq_cst)) return error.Cancelled;
  6745              std.Thread.yield() catch {};
  6746          }
  6747          return .{
  6748              .disposition = .accepted,
  6749              .body = try alloc.dupe(u8, "{\"access_token\":\"renewed-token\",\"refresh_token\":\"renewed-refresh\",\"expires_in\":3600,\"scope\":\"openid offline_access\",\"token_type\":\"Bearer\"}"),
  6750          };
  6751      }
  6752  };
  6753  
  6754  test "a prompt that leaves during its credential refresh lets the refresh finish and save the new login" {

  6756      const alloc = std.testing.allocator;

   878  fn commandInPath(alloc: Allocator, command_name: []const u8) !bool {
   879      const path_env = io_mod.getenv("PATH") orelse return false;
   880  
   881      return commandInPathValue(alloc, command_name, path_env);
   882  }
   883  
   884  fn commandInPathValue(alloc: Allocator, command_name: []const u8, path_env: []const u8) !bool {
   885      var it = std.mem.splitScalar(u8, path_env, std.Io.Dir.path.delimiter);
   886      while (it.next()) |entry| {
   887          if (entry.len == 0) continue;
   888  
   889          var candidate_buf: [std.Io.Dir.max_path_bytes]u8 = undefined;
   890          const candidate = std.mem.print(&candidate_buf, "{s}" ++ std.Io.Dir.path.sep_str ++ "{s}", .{ entry, command_name }) catch {
   891              const owned = try std.Io.Dir.path.join(alloc, &.{ entry, command_name });
   892              defer alloc.free(owned);
   893              if (pathExists(owned)) return true;
   894              continue;
   895          };
   896  
   897          if (pathExists(candidate)) return true;
   898  
   899          // Windows stores a program under an extension from `PATHEXT`, so `gh` on
   900          // the path is the file `gh.exe`, `gh.cmd`, `gh.bat`, or `gh.com`. Asking
   901          // for the bare name would report a program that is installed as missing.
   902          if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
   903              for (windows_program_extensions) |extension| {
   904                  var with_extension_buf: [std.Io.Dir.max_path_bytes]u8 = undefined;
   905                  const with_extension = std.mem.print(
   906                      &with_extension_buf,
   907                      "{s}" ++ std.Io.Dir.path.sep_str ++ "{s}{s}",
   908                      .{ entry, command_name, extension },
   909                  ) catch continue;
   910                  if (pathExists(with_extension)) return true;
   911              }
   912          }
   913      }
   914  
   915      return false;
   916  }
   917  
   918  /// The extensions Windows gives an executable, in the order `cmd` looks for
   919  /// them. A user's `PATHEXT` may add more; these cover the ones a program is
   920  /// installed under.
   921  const windows_program_extensions = [_][]const u8{ ".exe", ".cmd", ".bat", ".com" };
   922  
   923| =======
   924  >>>>>>> origin/main

   927          return accessPath(path) catch false;

   316  /// dots, such as `local.model_metadata.m.request.messages`, for the message
   317  /// that tells the person (`config_runtime.configuredProviderMessage`). A key
   318  /// that is not a plain word is quoted, and a long one is cut on a character
   319  /// boundary, so the path stays one short line.
   320  pub const FailurePath = struct {
   321      bytes: [max_bytes]u8 = undefined,
   322      len: usize = 0,
   323      starts: [max_depth]usize = undefined,
   324      depth: usize = 0,
   325  
   326      /// Deeper than any settings path: a connection, a model, a level, a field.
   327      const max_depth = 8;
   328      const max_key_bytes = 48;
   329      const cut_mark = "…";
   330      /// Every segment at its longest, so a path never runs out of room.
   331      const max_bytes = max_depth * (max_key_bytes + cut_mark.len + 3);
   332  
   333      pub fn text(self: *const FailurePath) []const u8 {
   334          return self.bytes[0..self.len];
   335      }
   336  
   337      fn push(self: *FailurePath, key: []const u8) void {
   338          if (self.depth < max_depth) self.starts[self.depth] = self.len;
   339          self.depth += 1;
   340          if (self.depth > max_depth) return;
   341          if (self.depth > 1) self.append(".");
   342          const plain = key.len > 0 and for (key) |byte| {
   343              if (!std.ascii.isAlphanumeric(byte) and byte != '_' and byte != '-') break false;
   344          } else true;
   345          if (!plain) self.append("\"");
   346          var end = @min(key.len, max_key_bytes);
   347          while (end < key.len and end > 0 and key[end] & 0xC0 == 0x80) end -= 1;
   348          for (key[0..end]) |byte| self.append(&.{if (byte < 0x20 or byte == 0x7f) '?' else byte});
   349          if (end < key.len) self.append(cut_mark);
   350          if (!plain) self.append("\"");
   351      }
   352  
   353      fn pop(self: *FailurePath) void {
   354          self.depth -= 1;
   355          if (self.depth < max_depth) self.len = self.starts[self.depth];
   356      }
   357  
   358      fn append(self: *FailurePath, part: []const u8) void {
   359          const room = @min(part.len, self.bytes.len - self.len);
   360          @memcpy(self.bytes[self.len..][0..room], part[0..room]);
   361          self.len += room;
   362  >>>>>>> origin/main
   363      }
   364  };
   365

  1675! <<<<<<< HEAD
  1676  /// Reads up to `out.len` bytes from a descriptor, returning how many arrived.
  1677  ///
  1678  /// `std.posix.read` cannot serve: it is a `@compileError` on Windows, where a
  1679  /// descriptor is a handle and reading goes through `std.Io.File`. That alone is
  1680  /// not enough for a console: Zig 0.17's Windows IO is built on `NtReadFile`,
  1681  /// which refuses a console handle the same way it refuses to write one, so an
  1682  /// interactive keystroke read never completes. A console is read with `ReadConsoleA`
  1683  /// here instead. Both paths return zero at end of file.
  1684  pub fn readFd(fd: std.posix.fd_t, out: []u8) !usize {
  1685      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .wasi) return error.Unexpected;
  1686      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
  1687          if (out.len == 0) return 0;
  1688          if (isConsoleHandle(fd)) return windowsReadSome(fd, out);
  1689      }
  1690      const file = fileFromHandle(fd);
  1691      return file.readStreaming(getIo(), &.{out}) catch |err| switch (err) {
  1692          error.EndOfStream => 0,
  1693          else => return err,
  1694      };
  1695  }
  1696  
  1697  fn fileFromHandle(fd: std.posix.fd_t) std.Io.File {
  1698      return .{ .handle = fd, .flags = .{ .nonblocking = handleIsAsynchronous(fd) } };
  1699  }
  1700  
  1701  fn handleIsAsynchronous(fd: std.posix.fd_t) bool {
  1702      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) return false;
  1703      const windows = std.os.windows;
  1704      var status: windows.IO_STATUS_BLOCK = undefined;
  1705      var mode: windows.FILE.MODE.INFORMATION = undefined;
  1706      // Inherited and child pipes can use overlapped I/O. Giving one to std.Io
  1707      // as a synchronous file reaches unreachable when a read or write is pending.
  1708      if (windows.ntdll.NtQueryInformationFile(fd, &status, &mode, @sizeOf(@TypeOf(mode)), .Mode) != .SUCCESS) return false;
  1709      return mode.Mode.IO == .ASYNCHRONOUS;
  1710  }
  1711  
  1712  test "Windows readFd handles an asynchronous pipe waiting for bytes and EOF" {

  1714      var threaded = std.Io.Threaded.init(std.testing.allocator, .{});
  1715      defer threaded.deinit();
  1716      const pipe = try threaded.windowsCreatePipe(.{
  1717          .server = .{ .mode = .{ .IO = .ASYNCHRONOUS } },
  1718          .client = .{ .mode = .{ .IO = .SYNCHRONOUS_NONALERT } },
  1719          .inbound = true,
  1720      });
  1721      defer closeFd(pipe[0]);
  1722      try std.testing.expect(handleIsAsynchronous(pipe[0]));
  1723      try std.testing.expect(!handleIsAsynchronous(pipe[1]));

  1728              writeFd(fd, &.{ 0xff, 0x80, 0x00 }) catch {};
  1729          }
  1730      };

  1732      defer thread.join();

  1735      try std.testing.expectEqualSlices(u8, &.{ 0xff, 0x80, 0x00 }, &buffer);

  1738  
  1739  /// Reads once from a console, returning what the console had.
  1740  ///
  1741  /// `error.NotAConsole` means the handle is not a console input buffer.
  1742  fn windowsReadSome(fd: std.posix.fd_t, out: []u8) !usize {
  1743      const want: u32 = @intCast(@min(out.len, @as(usize, std.math.maxInt(u32))));
  1744      var read_count: u32 = 0;
  1745      if (windows_console_write.ReadConsoleA(fd, out.ptr, want, &read_count, null) == 0) {
  1746          return switch (windows_console_write.GetLastError()) {
  1747              windows_console_write.error_invalid_handle => error.NotAConsole,
  1748              else => error.Unexpected,
  1749          };
  1750      }
  1751      return read_count;
  1752  }
  1753  
  1754  /// Reads until one byte arrives, for a caller that wants a single keystroke.
  1755  /// Returns zero at end of file.
  1756  pub fn readByte(fd: std.posix.fd_t, out: *[1]u8) !usize {
  1757      return readFd(fd, out);
  1758  }
  1759  
  1760  /// Writes all of `bytes` to a descriptor.
  1761  ///
  1762  /// The counterpart to `readFd`. `std.posix.write` is unavailable on Windows, so
  1763  /// this goes through `std.Io.File`. That is not enough on its own for a console:
  1764  /// Zig 0.17's Windows IO is built on `NtWriteFile`, which refuses a console
  1765  /// handle with `INVALID_HANDLE` (the source notes that console device names are
  1766  /// not handled yet), and that surfaces as `NotOpenForWriting`. A console handle
  1767  /// therefore takes `WriteConsoleW`, which is the call a console accepts.
  1768  pub fn writeFd(fd: std.posix.fd_t, bytes: []const u8) !void {
  1769      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .wasi) return error.Unexpected;
  1770      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
  1771          if (bytes.len == 0) return;
  1772          if (isConsoleHandle(fd)) return windowsWriteAll(fd, bytes);
  1773      }
  1774      const file = fileFromHandle(fd);
  1775      return file.writeStreamingAll(getIo(), bytes);
  1776  }
  1777  
  1778  /// Writes every byte with `WriteConsoleW`, in pieces as large as the call
  1779  /// accepts.
  1780  ///
  1781  /// The console API has two write calls, and only the wide one is dependable for
  1782  /// fx's output. `WriteConsoleA` transcodes through the console's ANSI code page,
  1783  /// which mangles the bytes of a multi-byte UTF-8 character and can drop control
  1784  /// bytes such as the `ESC` that begins every escape sequence. The wide call takes
  1785  /// UTF-16 directly, so fx decodes its UTF-8 and hands over code units.
  1786  ///
  1787  /// `error.NotAConsole` means the handle is not a console screen buffer, which is
  1788  /// the one case the caller handles differently. Everything else is a write that
  1789  /// did not happen.
  1790  fn windowsWriteAll(fd: std.posix.fd_t, bytes: []const u8) !void {
  1791      // The console takes UTF-16, so the batch is decoded into a stack buffer and
  1792      // handed over as one call. Sizing it at a quarter of the byte count is safe:
  1793      // UTF-8 never uses fewer than one byte per code unit, and the API caps a
  1794      // single call at 64 Ki code units anyway.
  1795      var wide_buffer: [4096]u16 = undefined;
  1796      const max_wide = @min(wide_buffer.len, 0xFFFF);
  1797  
  1798      var read_total: usize = 0;
  1799      while (read_total < bytes.len) {
  1800          // Decode as many whole characters as the buffer holds. A trailing
  1801          // partial character stays for the next pass, so a split never reaches
  1802          // the console as a broken code unit.
  1803          var used: usize = 0;
  1804          while (read_total < bytes.len) {
  1805              const remaining = bytes[read_total..];
  1806              const width = utf8Width(remaining[0]);
  1807              if (width == 0 or width > remaining.len) return error.Unexpected;
  1808              var scratch: [4]u16 = undefined;
  1809              const units = std.unicode.utf8ToUtf16Le(&scratch, remaining[0..width]) catch return error.Unexpected;
  1810              if (units == 0 or used + units > max_wide) break;
  1811              @memcpy(wide_buffer[used..][0..units], scratch[0..units]);
  1812              used += units;
  1813              read_total += width;
  1814          }
  1815          if (used == 0) return error.Unexpected;
  1816          var written: u32 = 0;
  1817          if (windows_console_write.WriteConsoleW(fd, &wide_buffer, @intCast(used), &written, null) == 0) {
  1818              const last_error = windows_console_write.GetLastError();
  1819              return switch (last_error) {
  1820                  windows_console_write.error_invalid_handle => error.NotAConsole,
  1821                  else => error.Unexpected,
  1822              };
  1823          }
  1824          if (written != used) return error.Unexpected;
  1825      }
  1826  }
  1827  
  1828  /// The byte length of the UTF-8 character that starts with `first`.
  1829  fn utf8Width(first: u8) usize {
  1830      return switch (first) {
  1831          0x00...0x7f => 1,
  1832          0xc0...0xdf => 2,
  1833          0xe0...0xef => 3,
  1834          0xf0...0xf7 => 4,
  1835          // A continuation byte has no width of its own, so this cannot be the
  1836          // start of a character.
  1837          else => 0,
  1838      };
  1839  }
  1840  
  1841  /// Whether a Windows handle names a console screen buffer rather than a file,
  1842  /// pipe, or socket.
  1843  ///
  1844  /// `GetConsoleMode` succeeds only for a console handle, which is the question
  1845  /// that decides whether a console-only call is needed.
  1846  /// How many bytes are ready to read on `fd` without blocking.
  1847  ///
  1848  /// A terminal that answers a query writes its reply into the input stream, and
  1849  /// a terminal that does not stays silent; without this the probe would park on
  1850  /// a read that never returns. A console reports its pending record count, a
  1851  /// pipe reports its buffered bytes, and anything else reports zero so the
  1852  /// caller falls back to its timeout.
  1853  pub fn bytesWaiting(fd: std.posix.fd_t) !usize {
  1854      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .wasi) return 0;
  1855      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) {
  1856          var fds = [_]std.posix.pollfd{.{ .fd = fd, .events = std.posix.POLL.IN, .revents = 0 }};
  1857          const ready = std.posix.poll(&fds, 0) catch return 0;
  1858          if (ready == 0) return 0;
  1859          return 1;
  1860      }
  1861      if (isConsoleHandle(fd)) {
  1862          var count: u32 = 0;
  1863          if (windows_console_input.GetNumberOfConsoleInputEvents(fd, &count) == 0) return 0;
  1864          return @intCast(count);
  1865      }
  1866      var available: u32 = 0;
  1867      if (windows_console_input.PeekNamedPipe(fd, null, 0, null, &available, null) == 0) return 0;
  1868      return @intCast(available);
  1869  }
  1870  
  1871  const windows_console_input = if (builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) struct {} else struct {
  1872      extern "kernel32" fn GetNumberOfConsoleInputEvents(handle: std.os.windows.HANDLE, count: *u32) callconv(.winapi) i32;
  1873      extern "kernel32" fn PeekNamedPipe(
  1874          pipe: std.os.windows.HANDLE,
  1875          buffer: ?*anyopaque,
  1876          buffer_size: u32,
  1877          bytes_read: ?*u32,
  1878          total_available: ?*u32,
  1879          bytes_left: ?*u32,
  1880      ) callconv(.winapi) i32;
  1881  };
  1882  
  1883  pub fn isConsoleHandle(fd: std.posix.fd_t) bool {
  1884      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) return false;
  1885      var mode: u32 = 0;
  1886      if (windows_console_write.GetConsoleMode(fd, &mode) != 0) return true;
  1887      // `GetConsoleMode` reads the expected error for whether the handle is a
  1888      // console at all. Logging it separates "not a console" from "console but
  1889      // the call failed", which look the same otherwise.
  1890      return false;
  1891  }
  1892  
  1893  const windows_console_write = if (builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) struct {} else struct {
  1894      /// The console write call. A console screen buffer accepts this and not the
  1895      /// file call, which is the difference that made every frame fail. The wide
  1896      /// form is used because the ANSI form transcodes through a code page.
  1897      extern "kernel32" fn WriteConsoleW(
  1898          console_output: std.posix.fd_t,
  1899          buffer: [*]const u16,
  1900          number_of_chars_to_write: u32,
  1901          number_of_chars_written: ?*u32,
  1902          reserved: ?*anyopaque,
  1903      ) callconv(.winapi) c_int;
  1904      /// The console read call, for the same reason.
  1905      extern "kernel32" fn ReadConsoleA(
  1906          console_input: std.posix.fd_t,
  1907          buffer: [*]u8,
  1908          number_of_chars_to_read: u32,
  1909          number_of_chars_read: ?*u32,
  1910          input_control: ?*anyopaque,
  1911      ) callconv(.winapi) c_int;
  1912      extern "kernel32" fn GetConsoleMode(h_console: std.posix.fd_t, mode: *u32) callconv(.winapi) c_int;
  1913      extern "kernel32" fn GetLastError() callconv(.winapi) u32;
  1914  
  1915      /// `ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE`, how a handle that is not a console answers.
  1916      const error_invalid_handle: u32 = 6;
  1917  };
  1918  
  1919  /// Closes a descriptor.
  1920  pub fn closeFd(fd: std.posix.fd_t) void {
  1921      const file: std.Io.File = .{ .handle = fd, .flags = .{ .nonblocking = false } };
  1922      file.close(getIo());
  1923  }
  1924  
  1925  /// Appends a line to `%TEMP%\fx-boot.log` when `FX_BOOT_LOG` is set.
  1926  ///
  1927  /// Startup has to be diagnosable on a machine where the console cannot be
  1928  /// written, because that is exactly when the interesting failure happens. So
  1929  /// this opens the log with the raw Windows call and writes through `WriteFile`,
  1930  /// using none of the IO that the startup path is still setting up.
  1931  pub fn bootLog(text: []const u8) void {
  1932      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) return;
  1933      const enabled = getenv("FX_BOOT_LOG") orelse return;
  1934      if (enabled.len == 0) return;
  1935      const alloc = std.heap.page_allocator;
  1936      const temp = tempDir(alloc) catch return;
  1937      defer alloc.free(temp);
  1938      const path = std.mem.concat(alloc, u8, &.{ temp, "\\fx-boot.log" }) catch return;
  1939      defer alloc.free(path);
  1940  
  1941      var wide_path: [windows_path_max_wide]u16 = undefined;
  1942      const wide_len = std.unicode.utf8ToUtf16Le(&wide_path, path) catch return;
  1943      if (wide_len + 1 > wide_path.len) return;
  1944      wide_path[wide_len] = 0;
  1945  
  1946      const handle = windows_boot.CreateFileW(
  1947          @ptrCast(&wide_path),
  1948          windows_boot.file_append_data,
  1949          windows_boot.file_share_write,
  1950          null,
  1951          windows_boot.open_always,
  1952          windows_boot.file_attribute_normal,
  1953          null,
  1954      ) orelse return;
  1955      defer _ = windows_boot.CloseHandle(handle);
  1956      var written: u32 = 0;
  1957      _ = windows_boot.WriteFile(handle, text.ptr, @intCast(@min(text.len, @as(usize, std.math.maxInt(u32)))), &written, null);
  1958  }
  1959  
  1960  /// Formats one boot-log line. See `bootLog`.
  1961  pub fn bootLogf(comptime fmt: []const u8, args: anytype) void {
  1962      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) return;
  1963      var buffer: [512]u8 = undefined;
  1964      const text = std.mem.print(&buffer, fmt, args) catch return;
  1965      bootLog(text);
  1966  }
  1967  
  1968  const windows_boot = if (builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) struct {} else struct {
  1969      const file_append_data: u32 = 0x00000004;
  1970      const file_share_write: u32 = 0x00000002;

  1993  /// Applies file permissions, on a platform that has them.
  1994  ///
  1995  /// A POSIX mode is meaningful and this forwards it. Windows has no mode: its
  1996  /// `Permissions` is a `DWORD` of file attributes, and Zig 0.17 does not implement
  1997  /// the directory half of the call at all, so `Dir.setPermissions` there reaches a
  1998  /// `@panic("TODO implement dirSetPermissionsWindows")` in its own standard
  1999  /// library. Since fx cannot set an ACL through this API anyway, the right answer
  2000  /// on Windows is to leave the inherited ACL alone rather than to crash.
  2001  /// Sets permissions on a file or a directory handle, on a platform that has
  2002  /// them.
  2003  ///
  2004  /// A POSIX mode is meaningful there and this forwards it. Windows has no mode:
  2005  /// its `Permissions` is a `DWORD` of file attributes, and Zig 0.17 does not
  2006  /// implement the directory half of the call at all, so `Dir.setPermissions` there
  2007  /// reaches a `@panic("TODO implement dirSetPermissionsWindows")` inside its own
  2008  /// standard library. fx cannot set an ACL through this API anyway, so the right
  2009  /// answer on Windows is to leave the inherited ACL alone rather than crash.
  2010  ///
  2011  /// One function serves both handle kinds because a caller often has either: `File`
  2012  /// for an open stream, `Dir` for a folder it just made.
  2013  pub fn setPermissions(handle: anytype, permissions: std.Io.File.Permissions) error{
  2014      PrivateStatePermissionsUnsupported,
  2015      AccessDenied,
  2016      Unexpected,
  2017  }!void {
  2018      if (comptime modeIsEnforced) {
  2019          handle.setPermissions(getIo(), permissions) catch |err| switch (err) {
  2020              error.AccessDenied => return error.AccessDenied,
  2021              else => return error.Unexpected,
  2022          };
  2023      } else {
  2024          _ = .{ handle, permissions };
  2025      }
  2026  }
  2027  
  2028  /// Sets the permissions of the entry `sub_path` inside `dir`, on a platform that
  2029  /// has them.
  2030  ///
  2031  /// The path form of `setFilePermissions`, for a caller that has a name rather
  2032  /// than an open handle. Windows reaches the same unimplemented directory call in
  2033  /// its own standard library, so it takes the same answer: leave the inherited ACL
  2034  /// alone rather than crash.
  2035  pub fn setPathPermissions(
  2036      dir: std.Io.Dir,
  2037      sub_path: []const u8,
  2038      permissions: std.Io.File.Permissions,
  2039      options: std.Io.Dir.SetPermissionsOptions,
  2040  ) !void {
  2041      if (comptime modeIsEnforced) {
  2042          try dir.setFilePermissions(getIo(), sub_path, permissions, options);
  2043      } else {
  2044          _ = .{ dir, sub_path, permissions, options };
  2045      }
  2046  }

  2048  /// Whether a file descriptor refers to a terminal.
  2049  ///
  2050  /// `std.c.isatty` cannot serve: Windows has no `STDIN_FILENO`, so its `fd_t` is
  2051  /// a `HANDLE` and a call that passes a descriptor number fails to compile.
  2052  /// `File.isTty` asks the platform the right question on both. Errors mean "not a
  2053  /// terminal", which is what every caller's `isatty == 0` test already did.
  2054  pub fn isTty(fd: std.posix.fd_t) bool {
  2055      const file: std.Io.File = .{ .handle = fd, .flags = .{ .nonblocking = false } };
  2056      return (file.isTty(getIo()) catch false);
  2057  }

  2074  /// The handle of a standard stream, for code that needs a descriptor value
  2075  /// rather than a `std.Io.File`.
  2076  ///
  2077  /// `std.posix.STDIN_FILENO` cannot be used across platforms: Windows declares
  2078  /// no such constant, because its handles are values a process asks the kernel
  2079  /// for rather than fixed small numbers. Zig also omits `GetStdHandle`, so fx
  2080  /// declares it. Every caller that used the POSIX constant takes its value here.
  2081  pub const StandardStream = enum { input, output, stderr };
  2082  
  2083  pub fn standardStreamHandle(stream: StandardStream) std.posix.fd_t {
  2084      return switch (stream) {
  2085          .input => stdinHandle(),
  2086          .output => stdoutHandle(),
  2087          .stderr => stderrHandle(),
  2088      };
  2089  }
  2090  
  2091  pub fn stdinHandle() std.posix.fd_t {
  2092      return resolveStandardHandle(.input, std.Io.File.stdin().handle);
  2093  }
  2094  
  2095  pub fn stdoutHandle() std.posix.fd_t {
  2096      return resolveStandardHandle(.output, std.Io.File.stdout().handle);
  2097  }
  2098  
  2099  pub fn stderrHandle() std.posix.fd_t {
  2100      return resolveStandardHandle(.stderr, std.Io.File.stderr().handle);
  2101  }
  2102  
  2103  /// Uses the handle the process environment block carries when it is real, and
  2104  /// asks the kernel when it is not.
  2105  ///
  2106  /// `std.Io.File.stdout()` and its siblings read the process environment block,
  2107  /// which holds the handles the parent passed in. A process started with a
  2108  /// console attached but a redirected stream, which is what a pseudo console and
  2109  /// a piping shell both do, can have a null handle there for a stream the kernel
  2110  /// still serves. `GetStdHandle` is the documented way to ask for the current
  2111  /// handle and answers for both cases.
  2112  fn resolveStandardHandle(stream: StandardStream, from_block: std.posix.fd_t) std.posix.fd_t {
  2113      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) return from_block;
  2114      if (isUsableHandle(from_block)) return from_block;
  2115      const id: u32 = switch (stream) {
  2116          .input => windows_std_input_handle,
  2117          .output => windows_std_output_handle,
  2118          .stderr => windows_std_error_handle,
  2119      };
  2120      const from_kernel = windows_stdio.GetStdHandle(id) orelse return from_block;
  2121      if (!isUsableHandle(from_kernel)) return from_block;
  2122      return from_kernel;
  2123  }
  2124  
  2125  /// Whether a Windows handle names a real object rather than the null or invalid
  2126  /// value a missing stream reports.
  2127  fn isUsableHandle(handle: std.posix.fd_t) bool {
  2128      if (@intFromPtr(handle) == 0) return false;
  2129      return @intFromPtr(handle) != std.math.maxInt(usize);
  2130  }
  2131  
  2132  const windows_std_input_handle: u32 = @bitCast(@as(i32, -10));
  2133  const windows_std_output_handle: u32 = @bitCast(@as(i32, -11));
  2134  const windows_std_error_handle: u32 = @bitCast(@as(i32, -12));
  2135  
  2136  const windows_stdio = if (builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) struct {} else struct {
  2137      extern "kernel32" fn GetStdHandle(std_handle: u32) callconv(.winapi) ?std.posix.fd_t;
  2138  };

  2157  /// A terminal's size in character cells.
  2158  pub const TerminalSize = struct {
  2159      rows: u16,
  2160      columns: u16,
  2161  };
  2162  
  2163  /// Reads a terminal's size.
  2164  ///
  2165  /// POSIX asks the kernel with `TIOCGWINSZ`. Windows has no such ioctl, so it
  2166  /// reads the console screen buffer's window with `GetConsoleScreenBufferInfo`.
  2167  /// A handle that is not a terminal, or a console with no window, returns
  2168  /// `error.NotATerminal` for the callers that treat that as "use a default".
  2169  pub fn terminalSize(fd: std.posix.fd_t) !TerminalSize {
  2170      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
  2171          var info: windows_console_info.CONSOLE_SCREEN_BUFFER_INFO = undefined;
  2172          if (windows_console_info.GetConsoleScreenBufferInfo(fd, &info) == 0) return error.NotATerminal;
  2173          const columns = info.srWindow.Right - info.srWindow.Left + 1;
  2174          const rows = info.srWindow.Bottom - info.srWindow.Top + 1;
  2175          if (columns <= 0 or rows <= 0) return error.NotATerminal;
  2176          return .{ .rows = @intCast(rows), .columns = @intCast(columns) };
  2177      }
  2178      if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .wasi) return error.NotATerminal;
  2179      var ws: std.posix.winsize = .{ .row = 0, .col = 0, .xpixel = 0, .ypixel = 0 };
  2180      const request: c_int = @intCast(std.c.T.IOCGWINSZ);
  2181      if (std.c.ioctl(fd, request, &ws) < 0) return error.NotATerminal;
  2182      if (ws.col == 0 or ws.row == 0) return error.NotATerminal;
  2183      return .{ .rows = ws.row, .columns = ws.col };
  2184  }
  2185  
  2186  const windows_console_info = if (builtin.target.os.tag != .windows) struct {} else struct {
  2187      const COORD = extern struct { X: i16, Y: i16 };
  2188      const SMALL_RECT = extern struct { Left: i16, Top: i16, Right: i16, Bottom: i16 };
  2189      const CONSOLE_SCREEN_BUFFER_INFO = extern struct {
  2190          dwSize: COORD,
  2191          dwCursorPosition: COORD,
  2192          wAttributes: u16,
  2193          srWindow: SMALL_RECT,
  2194          dwMaximumWindowSize: COORD,
  2195      };
  2196      extern "kernel32" fn GetConsoleScreenBufferInfo(
  2197          h_console_output: std.posix.fd_t,

  2224  /// Windows has no termios. A console's behavior is one mode word per handle,
  2225  /// not the four flag sets a POSIX `termios` carries, so fx keeps the distinction
  2226  /// it actually needs, "is this console in raw mode", in a `termios` value it
  2227  /// never reads field by field on this platform. The input mode word is stored in
  2228  /// the first control-character slot, and `windowsApplyConsoleMode` takes it back
  2229  /// out. Only `tcgetattr`, `tcsetattr`, and the raw-mode helper below touch it.
  2230  fn windowsConsoleAttributes(fd: std.posix.fd_t) std.posix.TermiosGetError!std.posix.termios {
  2231      const mode = try windowsGetConsoleMode(fd);
  2232      var term = std.mem.zeroes(std.posix.termios);
  2233      const bytes: [4]u8 = @bitCast(mode);
  2234      @memcpy(term.c_cc[0..4], &bytes);
  2235      return term;
  2236  }
  2237  
  2238  /// The Windows console mode fx reads and writes. A VT-capable console keeps
  2239  /// processed output and virtual terminal processing on; raw input turns off line
  2240  /// input, echo, and Ctrl+C processing, which is the part of raw mode a console
  2241  /// can express. Window input stays on so a resize still reaches fx.
  2242  const windows_raw_input_mode: u32 = windows_enable_window_input | windows_enable_virtual_terminal_input | windows_enable_extended_flags;
  2243  const windows_cooked_input_mode: u32 = windows_enable_processed_input |
  2244      windows_enable_line_input |

  2275  /// Whether the descriptor names a console rather than a redirected file.

  2282  /// Puts a Windows console into the mode fx draws with: raw input plus escape
  2283  /// processing on the output handle. The caller passes the value `tcgetattr`
  2284  /// returned so it can be restored.
  2285  pub fn windowsEnableRawConsole(
  2286      input_fd: std.posix.fd_t,
  2287      output_fd: std.posix.fd_t,
  2288      saved: std.posix.termios,
  2289  ) std.posix.TermiosSetError!void {
  2290      _ = saved;
  2291      try windowsSetConsoleMode(input_fd, windows_raw_input_mode);
  2292      var output_mode: u32 = 0;
  2293      if (WindowsConsole.GetConsoleMode(output_fd, &output_mode) == 0) return error.NotATerminal;
  2294      try windowsSetConsoleMode(
  2295          output_fd,
  2296          output_mode | windows_enable_processed_output | windows_enable_virtual_terminal_processing,
  2297      );
  2298  }

   334      /// Whether one more child would still be within `max_running`. Admission

   336      /// behind that never ran. Only slots still working count: a finished

   339      pub fn hasCapacity(self: *Owner) bool {

   350      /// its row in the agent list.
   351      pub fn currentCall(self: *Owner, child_id: []const u8) ?live_metrics.Call {
   352          self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io_mod.getIo());
   353          defer self.mutex.unlock(io_mod.getIo());

   628      /// The parent's `stop`: ends the child's running turn, which owes no
   629      /// report, since the stop call answers it. Returns how many of the
   630      /// parent's messages the turn leaves unread, which the stop drops, or
   631      /// null when nothing runs (`tests/tla/subagent/StopMessage.tla`
   632      /// NoSilentLoss).
   633      pub fn stop(self: *Owner, child_id: []const u8) ?usize {
   634          self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io_mod.getIo());
   635          defer self.mutex.unlock(io_mod.getIo());
   636          for (self.slots.items) |slot| {
   637              if (!std.mem.eql(u8, slot.child_id, child_id)) continue;
   638              if (slot.completion != .running) return null;
   639              // A second stop of a turn still ending: the first counted its
   640              // unread messages, which stay queued until the end drops them.
   641              const counted = slot.stopped_by_parent;
   642              slot.stopped_by_parent = true;
   643              slot.cancel.store(true, .seq_cst);
   644              stopTurn(slot, .cancel);
   645              if (counted) return 0;
   646              // Counted after the cancel, so no step of the turn reads one of
   647              // them any more (`StopMessage.tla` CountedNeverRead).
   648              return if (slot.worker) |worker| worker.queuedParentSteering() else 0;
   649          }
   650          return null;

   110  /// protocol. The wire shape is the one `openai_codex` already sends, minus the
   111  /// parts that only a subscription makes sense of: no stored conversation, no
   112  /// encrypted reasoning include, and no priority service tier.
   113  fn build_responses_request(
   114      alloc: Allocator,
   115      request: streams.RequestData,
   116      identity: model_provider.ProviderId,
   117  ) ![]u8 {
   118      try request.validatePrompt();

   124      const projected = try types.projectProviderReplay(alloc, request.messages, .{
   125          .provider = identity,
   126          .model = request.model,

   130      var instructions: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
   131      defer instructions.deinit();

   133          const text = instruction.content orelse continue;
   134          if (text.len == 0) continue;
   135          if (instructions.written().len > 0) try instructions.writer.writeAll("\n\n");
   136          try instructions.writer.writeAll(text);
   137      }

   139      var out: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(alloc);
   140      errdefer out.deinit();
   141      const writer = &out.writer;
   142      try writer.writeAll("{\"model\":");
   143      try std.json.Stringify.value(request.model, .{}, writer);
   144      try writer.writeAll(",\"store\":false,\"stream\":true");
   145      if (instructions.written().len > 0) {
   146          try writer.writeAll(",\"instructions\":");
   147          try std.json.Stringify.value(instructions.written(), .{}, writer);
   148      }
   149      try writer.writeAll(",\"input\":[");

   161      try writer.writeAll(",\"tool_choice\":");
   162      try std.json.Stringify.value(request.tool_choice.label(), .{}, writer);
   163      try writer.writeAll(",\"parallel_tool_calls\":true");

   170      // level the user picked never reaches the provider.

   173              try writer.writeAll(",\"reasoning\":{\"effort\":");
   174              try std.json.Stringify.value(effort.label(), .{}, writer);
   175              try writer.writeByte('}');
   176          }
   177      }
   178      try writer.writeAll("}");
   179      return out.toOwnedSlice();

   296          // The caller's deadline decides first. A request whose deadline passed

   307  fn expired(deadline: std.Io.Clock.Timestamp) bool {
   308      return !std.Io.Clock.Timestamp.compare(std.Io.Clock.Timestamp.now(io.getIo(), .awake), .lt, deadline);
   309  }
   310  
   311  fn phase_deadline(milliseconds: i64, caller: ?std.Io.Clock.Timestamp) std.Io.Clock.Timestamp {
   312      const phase = std.Io.Clock.Timestamp.fromNow(io.getIo(), .{ .clock = .awake, .raw = .fromMilliseconds(milliseconds) });
   313      if (caller) |deadline| if (std.Io.Clock.Timestamp.compare(deadline, .lt, phase)) return deadline;
   314      return phase;
   315  }

   317  fn post(alloc: Allocator, definition: *const definitions.Definition, request: streams.ModelRequest, token: ?[]const u8, payload: []const u8) !streams.Result {
   318      const url = try definition.chat_url(alloc);
   319      defer alloc.free(url);
   320      const authorization = if (token) |value| try alloc.print("Bearer {s}", .{value}) else null;
   321      defer if (authorization) |value| secret.zeroAndFree(alloc, value);
   322      var client: std.http.Client = .{ .allocator = alloc, .io = io.getIo() };
   323      defer client.deinit();
   324      var uri = try std.Uri.parse(url);
   325      uri.scheme = if (std.ascii.eqlIgnoreCase(uri.scheme, "https")) "https" else if (std.ascii.eqlIgnoreCase(uri.scheme, "http")) "http" else return error.UnsupportedUriScheme;
   326      const anthropic_headers = [_]std.http.Header{
   327          .{ .name = "accept", .value = "text/event-stream" },
   328          .{ .name = "anthropic-version", .value = anthropic.api_version },
   329          .{ .name = "x-api-key", .value = token orelse "" },
   330      };
   331      var operation = client_mod.PostOperation{
   332          .client = &client,
   333          .uri = uri,
   334          .authorization = if (definition.protocol == .@"anthropic-messages") null else authorization,
   335          .extra_headers = if (definition.protocol == .@"anthropic-messages") anthropic_headers[0..(if (token != null) @as(usize, 3) else 2)] else &.{.{ .name = "accept", .value = "text/event-stream" }},
   336      };
   337      try request.admission.admit();
   338      var opened = try client_mod.openBoundedPost(alloc, request.cancel_flag, phase_deadline(30_000, request.deadline), &operation);
   339      defer opened.deinit(alloc);
   340      const http = &opened.request.?;
   341      var watch: client_mod.ConnectionWatch = .init(.{
   342          .timeout_ms = 120_000,
   343          .stall_timeout_ms = client_mod.ConnectionWatch.bodyStallTimeoutMs(request.deadline),
   344      });
   345      defer watch.stop();
   346      if (http.connection) |connection| try watch.start(request.cancel_flag, request.deadline, connection.stream_writer.stream);
   347      // The 120 s head wait also bounds sending the request.
   348      if (watch.armHead()) |err| return err;
   349      http.transfer_encoding = .{ .content_length = payload.len };
   350      var buffer: [8192]u8 = undefined;
   351      if (request.cancel_flag.load(.seq_cst)) return error.Cancelled;
   352      request.delivery.markPossiblySent();
   353      var body = try http.sendBodyUnflushed(&buffer);
   354      try body.writer.writeAll(payload);
   355      try body.end();
   356      if (http.connection) |connection| try connection.flush();
   357      var response = http.receiveHead(&.{}) catch |err| return watch.failure(err);
   358      if (watch.commitHead()) |err| return err;
   359      if (response.head.status != .ok) {
   360          // An error body is read under its own 30 s bound.
   361          watch.stop();
   362          watch = .init(.{ .patient = true, .stall_timeout_ms = std.math.maxInt(i64) });
   363          if (http.connection) |connection| try watch.start(request.cancel_flag, phase_deadline(30_000, request.deadline), connection.stream_writer.stream);
   364      }
   365      var retry_after: ?u64 = null;
   366      var headers = response.head.iterateHeaders();
   367      while (headers.next()) |header| if (std.ascii.eqlIgnoreCase(header.name, "retry-after")) {
   368          retry_after = std.fmt.parseUnsigned(u64, std.mem.trim(u8, header.value, " \t"), 10) catch null;
   369          break;
   370      };
   371      var transfer: [64 * 1024]u8 = undefined;
   372      const reader = response.reader(&transfer);
   373      if (response.head.status != .ok) {
   374          var detail = reader.allocRemaining(alloc, .limited(64 * 1024)) catch |err| switch (err) {
   375              error.StreamTooLong => try alloc.dupe(u8, "Provider error response exceeded the local limit"),
   376              else => return err,
   377          };
   378          errdefer alloc.free(detail);
   379          if (token) |value| {
   380              const redacted = try codec.redact_error_detail(alloc, detail, value);
   381              alloc.free(detail);
   382              detail = redacted;
   383          }
   384  
   385          return .{ .failed = .{ .kind = client_mod.httpFailureKind(alloc, .fromHttpStatus(response.head.status), detail), .detail = detail, .retry_after_seconds = retry_after, .ownership = .owned } };
   386      }
   387      var result = (switch (definition.protocol) {
   388          .@"openai-chat-completions" => consume(alloc, reader, request, watch.progress()),
   389          .@"openai-responses" => consumeResponses(alloc, reader, request),
   390          .@"anthropic-messages" => consumeAnthropic(alloc, reader, request),
   391      }) catch |err| {
   392          const failure = watch.failure(err);
   393          return switch (failure) {
   394              error.ResponsesReasoningConflict,
   395              error.ResponsesTextConflict,
   396              error.ResponsesToolCallConflict,
   397              error.ResponsesOutputItemConflict,
   398              error.InvalidEvent,
   399              error.InvalidChunk,
   400              error.StreamIncomplete,
   401              error.IncompleteStream,
   402              => .{ .failed = .{ .kind = .server_error, .detail = try alloc.dupe(u8, "The provider returned an inconsistent response stream; retrying."), .ownership = .owned } },
   403              else => failure,
   404          };
   405      };
   406      if (watch.finish()) |err| {
   407          result.deinit(alloc);
   408          return err;
   409      }
   410      return result;
   411  }

   430  /// Parses a streamed Anthropic reply. Anthropic frames events as `event:` and
   431  /// `data:` lines and ends a message with `message_stop`, so the reducer reads
   432  /// the `data:` payloads in order and finishes when it sees that event.
   433  pub fn consumeAnthropic(alloc: Allocator, reader: *std.Io.Reader, request: streams.ModelRequest) !streams.Result {
   434      var reducer = anthropic.Reducer.init(alloc);
   435      defer reducer.deinit(alloc);
   436      var framing = sse.Reader{ .max_event_bytes = 1024 * 1024, .max_total_bytes = 32 * 1024 * 1024 };
   437      defer framing.deinit(alloc);
   438      var reasoning_emitted: usize = 0;
   439      while (try framing.next(alloc, reader, request.cancel_flag)) |payload| {
   440          const text_before = reducer.text.items.len;
   441          try reducer.applyJson(alloc, payload);
   442          if (reducer.text.items.len > text_before) request.events.emit(.{ .content_delta = reducer.text.items[text_before..] });
   443          var reasoning_offset: usize = 0;
   444          for (reducer.blocks.items) |block| {
   445              const thinking = block.thinking.items;
   446              const skip = reasoning_emitted -| reasoning_offset;
   447              if (skip < thinking.len) request.events.emit(.{ .reasoning_delta = thinking[skip..] });
   448              reasoning_offset += thinking.len;
   449          }
   450          reasoning_emitted = reasoning_offset;
   451          if (reducer.finished) break;
   452      }
   453      return reducer.finish(alloc, request.cancel_flag.load(.seq_cst));
   454  }

   533  /// Parses a streamed Responses reply. The shape differs enough from
   534  /// chat-completions - typed events, an output array, tool items that arrive as
   535  /// their own events - that the protocol has its own reducer, so the configured
   536  /// provider has to read it with that one rather than the chat decoder.
   537  pub fn consumeResponses(alloc: Allocator, reader: *std.Io.Reader, request: streams.ModelRequest) !streams.Result {
   538      var reducer = responses.Reducer.init(alloc);
   539      defer reducer.deinit(alloc);
   540      var reader_sse = sse.Reader{ .max_event_bytes = 32 * 1024 * 1024, .max_total_bytes = configured_stream_limits.aggregate_bytes };
   541      defer reader_sse.deinit(alloc);
   542  
   543      // `EventSink` already adapts its context to these callback shapes, which is
   544      // the same adapter the chat and Anthropic decoders use.
   545      const callbacks = responses.StreamCallbacks{
   546          .context = @ptrCast(@constCast(&request.events)),
   547          .on_content = streams.EventSink.onContentChunk,
   548          .on_tool_start = streams.EventSink.onLocalToolStart,
   549          .on_reasoning = streams.EventSink.onReasoningChunk,
   550          .on_tool_input = streams.EventSink.onToolInputChunk,
   551      };
   552  
   553      const cancel_flag = request.cancel_flag;
   554      const completion = completion: {
   555          while (reader_sse.next(alloc, reader, cancel_flag) catch |err| return err) |json_text| {
   556              if (std.mem.eql(u8, json_text, "[DONE]")) break;
   557              if (reducer.applyJson(
   558                  alloc,
   559                  json_text,
   560                  callbacks,
   561                  cancel_flag,
   562                  request.content_capture_limit,
   563                  configured_stream_limits,
   564              ) catch |err| return err) break;
   565          }
   566          break :completion reducer.finish(alloc, cancel_flag, configured_stream_limits) catch |err| return err;
   567      };
   568      return .{ .completed = .{ .completion = completion, .ownership = .owned } };
   569  }
